## Semantic Chunks
### Section: Introduction
- **Level**: `intro`
- **Hash**: `96a98a804f9c15d0611b079665acb400ad35d93684dc5746b53ad4582322a687`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/my-students-essay-appeared-all-at-once-did-they-use-ai` (Similarity: 0.9313)

```text
No. Pasting an entire essay into Google Docs does not, by itself, prove that a student used ChatGPT or another AI tool.

A large paste can certainly be worth investigating, especially if a nearly complete essay suddenly appears with little additional revision. But there are many legitimate reasons a student might paste an entire draft into Google Docs: drafting in Microsoft Word, using a personal Google Doc, writing in notes, or working across devices.

The better question is not: "Did this student paste an essay?" It is: "Where did the pasted essay come from, and does the student's explanation match the other available evidence?"

Checkmark Plagiarism's essay writing playback helps teachers examine how assignments develop alongside AI detection , static AI detection, plagiarism detection , autograding , and integrations with Canvas and Google Classroom .
```

### Section: Why Does a Full-Essay Paste Look Suspicious?
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `46789bc8a62d30624c61588673c98c4ba5da9b9cbb1b3b686d7d931ec396921d`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-tell-if-a-student-actually-wrote-their-essay` (Similarity: 0.8638)

```text
Opening a document to find a title and two lines followed by an instant 1,500-word polished essay with complete citations naturally raises questions. However, the history only proves: a large amount of text entered this document at once —it does not prove ChatGPT generated that text .
```

### Section: What Could a Full-Essay Paste Mean?
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `668a6d507594ed467b762a7e70784bc52b8efda0b51763cc2b8208a345f9ea52`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/should-students-submit-their-google-docs-revision-history` (Similarity: 0.8616)

```text
Drafting in Microsoft Word: Many students prefer Word or desktop apps and paste the final draft into Google Docs solely for classroom submission. 
 Personal Google Doc: A student may draft in a personal account before copying to the school-assigned document. 
 Notes & Markdown Apps: Brainstorming and drafting in Notion, Apple Notes, or Obsidian before transferring. 
 Working Across Devices: Transferring work between home desktops, phones, and school Chromebooks. 
 Generative AI: The text may have been generated by ChatGPT or another LLM.
```

### Section: A Paste Shows Document Behavior, Not Authorship
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `0ba8e346bcc37fb754b07f93c5fd48e3b6d3a019e63f981e7b15775e6730dc2e`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-i-tell-if-a-student-actually-wrote-their-essay` (Similarity: 0.8624)

```text
A paste event indicates that content was imported rather than drafted directly in that browser tab. The appropriate next step is neutral inquiry: "I noticed most of the essay was added to this document at once. Where did you write the original draft?"
```

### Section: What If the Student Says They Wrote It in Word?
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `b1cf3789ab7b12ab87eb9cc81521d0a565598a9606f4ce281644529c44226c7a`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/can-teachers-see-if-students-used-chatgpt` (Similarity: 0.8820)

```text
That is a common, legitimate workflow. Ask whether the original Word document is available. An authentic earlier draft often shows multiple revision timestamps, notes, thesis changes, and gradual development, completely resolving the paste inquiry.
```

### Section: Compare the Essay With Previous Student Writing
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `1e4e74b297cc5c6046630500fbb77d3e0b486c38a0ea8acbf28b7565d6a9d58e`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/what-should-i-do-if-a-student-cant-explain-their-own-essay` (Similarity: 0.9308)

Compare vocabulary, sentence complexity, tone, grammar, and analysis with previous student work. If a pasted essay also sounds radically different from all past work, ask: "This essay is written differently from your previous assignments. What did you do differently this time?" ```

### Section: What Does a Strongly Concerning Pattern Look Like?
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `48e56ee707c301d37987c785c8801874043224c6061f103f076e96ad73fa851e`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/how-can-teachers-use-draft-history-to-investigate-ai-use` (Similarity: 0.9652)

```text
Concerning Corroborating Pattern

1,500-word essay pasted in a single timestamp 
 Zero subsequent edits or revisions 
 Writing style diverges completely from past work 
 AI detection raises severe flags on pasted text 
 Multiple citations do not exist in academic databases 
 Student cannot explain arguments or source origins

Valid External Drafting Pattern

1,500-word essay pasted from external Word doc 
 Student provides earlier Word draft with multi-session logs 
 Student fluently explains thesis, terms, & citations 
 Citations are fully verified in academic databases 
 Explanation completely resolves the initial paste question
```

### Section: A Practical Process for Reviewing a Full-Essay Paste
- **Level**: `h2`
- **Hash**: `14a20c8f04e81f22da78516bac2e32c9ca6a478f143c11594671ac03db27a952`
- **Best Competitor Match**: `/learning/2026/8/does-copying-and-pasting-automatically-mean-a-student-cheated` (Similarity: 0.8875)

```text
A Practical 13-Step Full-Essay Paste Review Process:

1. Confirm what document history and writing playback actually show. 
 2. Avoid assuming the inserted text automatically originated from AI. 
 3. Ask the student neutrally where the original draft was written. 
 4. Give the student an opportunity to provide earlier draft files or version logs. 
 5. Compare the submission against previous student work baselines. 
 6. Ask the student to explain the thesis and key argumentative passages. 
 7. Verify cited sources, author names, and direct quotes in academic databases. 
 8. Review AI detection reports as one corroborating signal among several. 
 9. Check plagiarism detection results for traditional web/database source matches. 
 10. Inquire neutrally about generative AI tools, writing assistants, and tutoring support. 
 11. Compare the student's explanation with the observable document history. 
 12. Determine whether any outside assistance violated published course policy. 
 13. Follow established school academic-integrity procedures if concerns persist.
```
